It’s more that I’m picking up on details that other people dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Have you talked to others about your concerns with the building, and do they share your view or do they seem to disagree with you about what’s happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve talked to people, though not as many as you might think, because you learn pretty quickly who is actually listening and who is just trying to manage you. I brought it up with the property manager first, obviously, because the mail being “accidentally” misdelivered over and over again was not normal. Then there were the noises in the walls — always at these particular times, like after I’d made a complaint or when I was trying to sleep — and neighbors making these little comments in the hallway that were just a bit too specific.\n\nMost people don’t come right out and agree, no. They’ll say things like, “Oh, mail gets mixed up,” or “Old buildings make noise,” or they’ll shrug and act like they don’t know what I mean. But that’s part of the problem. They want it to seem ordinary if you take each thing separately. I’ve tried to explain that it’s the pattern over months that matters. One mistake is one thing. Two, maybe. But when it’s the same kinds of incidents, with the same people around it, and every time there’s some neat little explanation or piece of paperwork to cover it, then at a certain point you have to stop pretending it’s coincidence.\n\nSo, no, they don’t really share my view openly. Some disagree, or say I’m reading too much into it. But I think some of them know more than they admit. They just don’t want to be involved, or they’re afraid of the manager, or they’re part of it. That’s why I’m here — because I need someone outside the building to understand that this has been going on for months, and no amount of their bland explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a coordinated effort or plan by the property manager and others in the building to make you leave, and if so, how do you know this is happening?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t really see how else to describe it at this point.\n\nI know that sounds like a strong word — “coordinated” — but when something happens once, fine, you can call it an accident. When the mail is misdelivered again and again, when the noises in the walls seem to come at particular times, and then certain neighbors make these little comments that line up with what’s been going on, it stops looking random. It’s been months of this.\n\nAnd the property manager always has some explanation ready. Paperwork, routine maintenance, “these things happen,” that kind of thing. But that’s exactly how they keep it deniable. Each separate thing can be explained away if you isolate it, but I’m not looking at one separate thing. I’m looking at the pattern.\n\nI worked as a management analyst for years. Patterns are what I notice. And this pattern is pressure — not direct, not written in a letter saying “leave,” obviously — but pressure to make living there uncomfortable enough that I’ll give up the apartment. The mail, the timed noises, the little comments, the bland explanations afterward… all of it fits together too neatly. No amount of their paperwork changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the property manager and others are targeting you for a specific reason, such as something about who you are, your background, or your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about my background in the usual sense, no. I mean, I’m not saying it’s because of my age or where I’m from or anything like that. It’s more specific than that. I think it’s because I notice things, and because I don’t just let sloppy explanations pass when the pattern doesn’t make sense.\n\nOnce I started pointing out the mail problem, and then the noises, and then the way certain neighbors would make these little comments that were just a little too well-timed, I think I became inconvenient. That’s the simplest way to put it. They want me out of the apartment, and they don’t want to do it directly because then there would be a record. So instead it’s this steady pressure — misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, comments in the hallway, and then the property manager produces some paperwork or bland explanation to make each incident look ordinary.\n\nBut I’m telling you, over months, it isn’t ordinary. You can dress it up however you want, but the pattern is still there. I’m here because I need someone to understand that. It’s not one thing. It’s the accumulation of things, and no amount of their explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe this coordinated effort against you is part of a larger plan or organization that extends beyond just the people in your building?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would say it’s some large organization, if that’s what you mean. I’m not claiming there’s a whole outside group with meetings and letterhead or anything like that. What I can say is what I’ve actually seen in the building, over and over, for months.\n\nThe property manager and certain people there are involved enough for it to be coordinated. That’s the word I keep coming back to, because the mail being misdelivered repeatedly, the noises in the walls at particular times, and then those little comments from neighbors — comments that seem casual if you weren’t paying attention, but are just too specific — all of that fits together. And then afterward there’s always some explanation, some paperwork, some “routine” reason to make it look normal.\n\nSo beyond the building? I can’t prove that, and I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But inside the building, yes, absolutely, there is a pattern. They’re trying to pressure me out of the apartment without saying it directly. And no amount of bland explanations changes what I can see when I look at all of it together.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your situation in the building is part of something bigger, like being watched or targeted because of something you know or who you are in a more significant way than just being an inconvenience to the property manager?\n\nPatient: No, not in that larger sense. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that I know some secret, or that there’s some outside group watching me because of who I am. That’s not what I’m trying to say.\n\nWhat I am saying is narrower, but it’s still very real. I’m here because for months now the property manager and certain people in the building have been using these things — the mail being misdelivered, the noises in the walls that happen at particular times, the little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s been happening — to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd every time I raise it, there’s an explanation ready. Paperwork, routine issue, mistake, maintenance, some perfectly bland answer. But those explanations only work if you look at each incident by itself. I’m not looking at one envelope or one noise or one hallway comment. I’m looking at the pattern over months.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it something bigger like I’m being targeted because I know something important. I think I became inconvenient to them, and now they’re trying to make living there uncomfortable enough that I leave on my own. That’s the part I need someone to understand. Their paperwork doesn’t change the pattern I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the property manager or others in the building have abilities or powers that others don’t, like being able to control events in ways that seem impossible, or know things they couldn’t normally know?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t put it that way. I’m not saying they have powers or some impossible ability. I don’t think the property manager can control things supernaturally or read my mind or anything like that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more practical, but it’s still deliberate. They’re in a position where they can affect things around me — mail, maintenance, access to certain areas, communication with neighbors, that kind of thing. And when you have a property manager and certain people in the building acting together, they don’t need special powers. They just need coordination.\n\nThat’s what I keep trying to explain. For months now, it’s been misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, and those little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s going on. Then when I ask about it or push back, suddenly there’s paperwork, or a routine explanation, or some excuse that makes each thing sound harmless if you isolate it.\n\nBut I’m not looking at each thing in isolation. I’m looking at the pattern. And no, that doesn’t require impossible abilities. It requires access, timing, and people willing to make my life uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. That’s what I believe is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the actions taken against you by the property manager and others in the building are planned and intentional, even if they may seem minor or explainable on their own?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, I do believe that. And I know someone could take any one thing I’m talking about and say, “Well, mail gets misdelivered,” or “Buildings make noises,” or “Neighbors say odd things sometimes.” I understand that. I’m not naïve about that.\n\nBut this has been going on for months, and it’s the way the pieces line up that matters. The mail doesn’t just go missing or end up somewhere else once. The noises in the walls happen at particular times, not just randomly. And then there are these little comments from people in the building — small things, things they can pretend are nothing — but they connect too closely to what’s been happening.\n\nThat’s why I say it’s intentional. Not because each incident is dramatic on its own, but because the pattern is too consistent. And every time I raise it, there’s paperwork or some routine explanation ready to make it sound normal. That’s part of it too, in my view. They want each piece to look minor so I sound unreasonable if I complain.\n\nBut I can see what they’re doing. The property manager and certain people in the building are using these things to pressure me out of the apartment without openly saying that’s what they’re doing. No amount of explanations changes that pattern for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one month where you experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or having thoughts that are very disorganized or hard to follow, and this wasn’t due to substance use or another medical condition?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d describe anything in those terms. I mean, “hearing voices” or “seeing things” — no, not in the way I think you mean, like some separate voice talking to me or seeing a person standing there who isn’t there. That’s not really what this is about.\n\nAs far as my thoughts being disorganized, I don’t think so. I know I can go into a lot of detail, and maybe I circle back because people keep missing the point, but to me it’s connected. I’m following a sequence of events. Other people may not want to see the connection, but that’s different from my thoughts not making sense.\n\nAnd I don’t use substances in a way that would explain anything like that. I’m just trying to be precise because when I give a short answer, people act like I’m leaving something out, and when I give the whole explanation, they say I’m overexplaining. So… no, I don’t believe I’ve had a month like what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events happening around you — like the noises, misdelivered mail, or things people say — have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “special” exactly, because that makes it sound mystical or like I’m imagining symbols in everything. That’s not what I mean. But do I believe those things are directed at me? Yes. Absolutely.\n\nThe misdelivered mail, the noises in the walls at certain times, the little comments from neighbors that seem casual but line up too neatly with what’s been happening — those aren’t just random to me. Other people may look at each one separately and say, “Oh, that’s nothing,” but that’s the problem. They’re designed to look like nothing if you take them one at a time.\n\nTo me the meaning is pretty clear: the property manager and certain people in the building are trying to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. They don’t say it openly, of course. There’s always some paperwork or routine explanation ready if I question it. But the pattern has been going on for months, and I can see it.\n\nSo yes, even if other people don’t see it, I believe those events are directed at me. Not in some supernatural way, but in a coordinated, intentional way.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that others may not see the pattern the way you do, and do you think it’s possible that these events are not connected the way you believe?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that other people may not see it, yes. This has been going on for months, and it’s the timing and the repetition and the way the explanations are always ready afterward. The property manager and certain people in the building can point to paperwork or routine procedures and make each piece sound normal, but when you’re the one living there, day after day, you can see how it all fits together.\n\nSo do I think it’s possible they’re not connected? Honestly, no — not in any real way. I can say, intellectually, that other people may not see the pattern because they aren’t experiencing the whole thing. But for me, the pattern is clear. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I do feel that way, though again I want to be careful with the phrase “special meaning,” because that makes it sound like I’m attaching some kind of fantasy meaning to ordinary things. To me it’s more practical than that. It’s not that every object or every event in the world is about me. I’m not saying that.

What I’m saying is that in my building, for months now, there have been these repeated things — mail going to the wrong place or showing up in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls at certain times, neighbors making small comments that seem casual if you don’t know the background — and they line up too specifically with what’s been happening. I’m here because I believe the property manager and certain people in the building are using those things to pressure me out of the apartment.

And when I bring it up, there’s always some explanation ready. Paperwork, maintenance, “oh, that was a mistake,” “oh, that’s just normal building noise,” whatever it is. But the explanations don’t change the pattern. If anything, the way they always have an explanation ready is part of what makes it so obvious to me.

So yes, I do think certain actions by others are directed at me. Not everything, not random things on the street, but the things happening around where I live — yes. Those are not just unrelated events to me. They’re connected, and they’re meant to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave.

I came in because this situation in my building has gone on for months now, and I’m at the point where I need someone to understand that it isn’t just a misunderstanding or me being “sensitive,” which is what they’d like people to think.\n\nThe property manager and certain people in the building have been putting pressure on me to leave my apartment. Not directly, of course — they’re too careful for that — but through things like my mail being misdelivered in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls that happen at very particular times, and neighbors making these little comments that connect to things they shouldn’t know or that clearly refer to what’s been happening. And then when I ask about it, there’s always paperwork or some bland explanation, like it’s all routine or accidental. But when you look at the pattern over time, it’s obvious. Months of it. No amount of their explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard sounds or voices that others around you don’t seem to hear, or seen things that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it that way, no. I mean, I hear noises, but these are real noises in the building — pipes, tapping, things in the walls, footsteps, doors, that kind of thing. It’s not like I’m hearing some voice talking to me when nobody is there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAnd seeing things… no, I don’t think so. I notice things other people don’t notice, but that’s different. Some people are just not paying attention, or they don’t want to get involved. I’m fairly observant — I was an analyst for years, so patterns stand out to me. But I’m not seeing, you know, figures or objects that aren’t there. It’s more that I’m picking up on details that other people dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Have you talked to others about your concerns with the building, and do they share your view or do they seem to disagree with you about what’s happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve talked to people, though not as many as you might think, because you learn pretty quickly who is actually listening and who is just trying to manage you. I brought it up with the property manager first, obviously, because the mail being “accidentally” misdelivered over and over again was not normal. Then there were the noises in the walls — always at these particular times, like after I’d made a complaint or when I was trying to sleep — and neighbors making these little comments in the hallway that were just a bit too specific.\n\nMost people don’t come right out and agree, no. They’ll say things like, “Oh, mail gets mixed up,” or “Old buildings make noise,” or they’ll shrug and act like they don’t know what I mean. But that’s part of the problem. They want it to seem ordinary if you take each thing separately. I’ve tried to explain that it’s the pattern over months that matters. One mistake is one thing. Two, maybe. But when it’s the same kinds of incidents, with the same people around it, and every time there’s some neat little explanation or piece of paperwork to cover it, then at a certain point you have to stop pretending it’s coincidence.\n\nSo, no, they don’t really share my view openly. Some disagree, or say I’m reading too much into it. But I think some of them know more than they admit. They just don’t want to be involved, or they’re afraid of the manager, or they’re part of it. That’s why I’m here — because I need someone outside the building to understand that this has been going on for months, and no amount of their bland explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a coordinated effort or plan by the property manager and others in the building to make you leave, and if so, how do you know this is happening?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t really see how else to describe it at this point.\n\nI know that sounds like a strong word — “coordinated” — but when something happens once, fine, you can call it an accident. When the mail is misdelivered again and again, when the noises in the walls seem to come at particular times, and then certain neighbors make these little comments that line up with what’s been going on, it stops looking random. It’s been months of this.\n\nAnd the property manager always has some explanation ready. Paperwork, routine maintenance, “these things happen,” that kind of thing. But that’s exactly how they keep it deniable. Each separate thing can be explained away if you isolate it, but I’m not looking at one separate thing. I’m looking at the pattern.\n\nI worked as a management analyst for years. Patterns are what I notice. And this pattern is pressure — not direct, not written in a letter saying “leave,” obviously — but pressure to make living there uncomfortable enough that I’ll give up the apartment. The mail, the timed noises, the little comments, the bland explanations afterward… all of it fits together too neatly. No amount of their paperwork changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the property manager and others are targeting you for a specific reason, such as something about who you are, your background, or your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about my background in the usual sense, no. I mean, I’m not saying it’s because of my age or where I’m from or anything like that. It’s more specific than that. I think it’s because I notice things, and because I don’t just let sloppy explanations pass when the pattern doesn’t make sense.\n\nOnce I started pointing out the mail problem, and then the noises, and then the way certain neighbors would make these little comments that were just a little too well-timed, I think I became inconvenient. That’s the simplest way to put it. They want me out of the apartment, and they don’t want to do it directly because then there would be a record. So instead it’s this steady pressure — misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, comments in the hallway, and then the property manager produces some paperwork or bland explanation to make each incident look ordinary.\n\nBut I’m telling you, over months, it isn’t ordinary. You can dress it up however you want, but the pattern is still there. I’m here because I need someone to understand that. It’s not one thing. It’s the accumulation of things, and no amount of their explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe this coordinated effort against you is part of a larger plan or organization that extends beyond just the people in your building?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would say it’s some large organization, if that’s what you mean. I’m not claiming there’s a whole outside group with meetings and letterhead or anything like that. What I can say is what I’ve actually seen in the building, over and over, for months.\n\nThe property manager and certain people there are involved enough for it to be coordinated. That’s the word I keep coming back to, because the mail being misdelivered repeatedly, the noises in the walls at particular times, and then those little comments from neighbors — comments that seem casual if you weren’t paying attention, but are just too specific — all of that fits together. And then afterward there’s always some explanation, some paperwork, some “routine” reason to make it look normal.\n\nSo beyond the building? I can’t prove that, and I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But inside the building, yes, absolutely, there is a pattern. They’re trying to pressure me out of the apartment without saying it directly. And no amount of bland explanations changes what I can see when I look at all of it together.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your situation in the building is part of something bigger, like being watched or targeted because of something you know or who you are in a more significant way than just being an inconvenience to the property manager?\n\nPatient: No, not in that larger sense. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that I know some secret, or that there’s some outside group watching me because of who I am. That’s not what I’m trying to say.\n\nWhat I am saying is narrower, but it’s still very real. I’m here because for months now the property manager and certain people in the building have been using these things — the mail being misdelivered, the noises in the walls that happen at particular times, the little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s been happening — to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd every time I raise it, there’s an explanation ready. Paperwork, routine issue, mistake, maintenance, some perfectly bland answer. But those explanations only work if you look at each incident by itself. I’m not looking at one envelope or one noise or one hallway comment. I’m looking at the pattern over months.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it something bigger like I’m being targeted because I know something important. I think I became inconvenient to them, and now they’re trying to make living there uncomfortable enough that I leave on my own. That’s the part I need someone to understand. Their paperwork doesn’t change the pattern I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the property manager or others in the building have abilities or powers that others don’t, like being able to control events in ways that seem impossible, or know things they couldn’t normally know?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t put it that way. I’m not saying they have powers or some impossible ability. I don’t think the property manager can control things supernaturally or read my mind or anything like that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more practical, but it’s still deliberate. They’re in a position where they can affect things around me — mail, maintenance, access to certain areas, communication with neighbors, that kind of thing. And when you have a property manager and certain people in the building acting together, they don’t need special powers. They just need coordination.\n\nThat’s what I keep trying to explain. For months now, it’s been misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, and those little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s going on. Then when I ask about it or push back, suddenly there’s paperwork, or a routine explanation, or some excuse that makes each thing sound harmless if you isolate it.\n\nBut I’m not looking at each thing in isolation. I’m looking at the pattern. And no, that doesn’t require impossible abilities. It requires access, timing, and people willing to make my life uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. That’s what I believe is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the actions taken against you by the property manager and others in the building are planned and intentional, even if they may seem minor or explainable on their own?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, I do believe that. And I know someone could take any one thing I’m talking about and say, “Well, mail gets misdelivered,” or “Buildings make noises,” or “Neighbors say odd things sometimes.” I understand that. I’m not naïve about that.\n\nBut this has been going on for months, and it’s the way the pieces line up that matters. The mail doesn’t just go missing or end up somewhere else once. The noises in the walls happen at particular times, not just randomly. And then there are these little comments from people in the building — small things, things they can pretend are nothing — but they connect too closely to what’s been happening.\n\nThat’s why I say it’s intentional. Not because each incident is dramatic on its own, but because the pattern is too consistent. And every time I raise it, there’s paperwork or some routine explanation ready to make it sound normal. That’s part of it too, in my view. They want each piece to look minor so I sound unreasonable if I complain.\n\nBut I can see what they’re doing. The property manager and certain people in the building are using these things to pressure me out of the apartment without openly saying that’s what they’re doing. No amount of explanations changes that pattern for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one month where you experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or having thoughts that are very disorganized or hard to follow, and this wasn’t due to substance use or another medical condition?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d describe anything in those terms. I mean, “hearing voices” or “seeing things” — no, not in the way I think you mean, like some separate voice talking to me or seeing a person standing there who isn’t there. That’s not really what this is about.\n\nAs far as my thoughts being disorganized, I don’t think so. I know I can go into a lot of detail, and maybe I circle back because people keep missing the point, but to me it’s connected. I’m following a sequence of events. Other people may not want to see the connection, but that’s different from my thoughts not making sense.\n\nAnd I don’t use substances in a way that would explain anything like that. I’m just trying to be precise because when I give a short answer, people act like I’m leaving something out, and when I give the whole explanation, they say I’m overexplaining. So… no, I don’t believe I’ve had a month like what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events happening around you — like the noises, misdelivered mail, or things people say — have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “special” exactly, because that makes it sound mystical or like I’m imagining symbols in everything. That’s not what I mean. But do I believe those things are directed at me? Yes. Absolutely.\n\nThe misdelivered mail, the noises in the walls at certain times, the little comments from neighbors that seem casual but line up too neatly with what’s been happening — those aren’t just random to me. Other people may look at each one separately and say, “Oh, that’s nothing,” but that’s the problem. They’re designed to look like nothing if you take them one at a time.\n\nTo me the meaning is pretty clear: the property manager and certain people in the building are trying to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. They don’t say it openly, of course. There’s always some paperwork or routine explanation ready if I question it. But the pattern has been going on for months, and I can see it.\n\nSo yes, even if other people don’t see it, I believe those events are directed at me. Not in some supernatural way, but in a coordinated, intentional way.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that others may not see the pattern the way you do, and do you think it’s possible that these events are not connected the way you believe?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that other people may not see it, yes. I mean, I’m sitting here talking to you because I understand that from the outside, if someone just hears “misdelivered mail” or “noises in the walls” or “a neighbor made a comment,” they can say, “Well, those things happen.” I do understand that.\n\nBut the issue is that they’re not isolated things to me. This has been going on for months, and it’s the timing and the repetition and the way the explanations are always ready afterward. The property manager and certain people in the building can point to paperwork or routine procedures and make each piece sound normal, but when you’re the one living there, day after day, you can see how it all fits together.\n\nSo do I think it’s possible they’re not connected? Honestly, no — not in any real way. I can say, intellectually, that other people may not see the pattern because they aren’t experiencing the whole thing. But for me, the pattern is clear. It’s been a pressure campaign to make me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without them having to say that directly. And no amount of their explanations changes what I can see happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events, objects, or actions by others — even things that aren’t directly related to you — have a special meaning or are directed at you in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do feel that way, though again I want to be careful with the phrase “special meaning,” because that makes it sound like I’m attaching some kind of fantasy meaning to ordinary things. To me it’s more practical than that. It’s not that every object or every event in the world is about me. I’m not saying that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is that in my building, for months now, there have been these repeated things — mail going to the wrong place or showing up in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls at certain times, neighbors making small comments that seem casual if you don’t know the background — and they line up too specifically with what’s been happening. I’m here because I believe the property manager and certain people in the building are using those things to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd when I bring it up, there’s always some explanation ready. If anything, the way they always have an explanation ready is part of what makes it so obvious to me.\n\nSo yes, I do think certain actions by others are directed at me. Not everything, not random things on the street, but the things happening around where I live — yes. Those are not just unrelated events to me. They’re connected, and they’re meant to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Other people may not want to see the connection, but that’s different from my thoughts not making sense.\n\nAnd I don’t use substances in a way that would explain anything like that. I’m just trying to be precise because when I give a short answer, people act like I’m leaving something out, and when I give the whole explanation, they say I’m overexplaining. So… no, I don’t believe I’ve had a month like what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events happening around you — like the noises, misdelivered mail, or things people say — have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “special” exactly, because that makes it sound mystical or like I’m imagining symbols in everything. That’s not what I mean. But do I believe those things are directed at me? Yes. Absolutely.\n\nThe misdelivered mail, the noises in the walls at certain times, the little comments from neighbors that seem casual but line up too neatly with what’s been happening — those aren’t just random to me. Other people may look at each one separately and say, “Oh, that’s nothing,” but that’s the problem. They’re designed to look like nothing if you take them one at a time.\n\nTo me the meaning is pretty clear: the property manager and certain people in the building are trying to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. They don’t say it openly, of course. There’s always some paperwork or routine explanation ready if I question it. But the pattern has been going on for months, and I can see it.\n\nSo yes, even if other people don’t see it, I believe those events are directed at me. Not in some supernatural way, but in a coordinated, intentional way.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that others may not see the pattern the way you do, and do you think it’s possible that these events are not connected the way you believe?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that other people may not see it, yes. I mean, I’m sitting here talking to you because I understand that from the outside, if someone just hears “misdelivered mail” or “noises in the walls” or “a neighbor made a comment,” they can say, “Well, those things happen.” I do understand that.\n\nBut the issue is that they’re not isolated things to me. This has been going on for months, and it’s the timing and the repetition and the way the explanations are always ready afterward. The property manager and certain people in the building can point to paperwork or routine procedures and make each piece sound normal, but when you’re the one living there, day after day, you can see how it all fits together.\n\nSo do I think it’s possible they’re not connected? Honestly, no — not in any real way. I can say, intellectually, that other people may not see the pattern because they aren’t experiencing the whole thing. But for me, the pattern is clear. It’s been a pressure campaign to make me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without them having to say that directly. And no amount of their explanations changes what I can see happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events, objects, or actions by others — even things that aren’t directly related to you — have a special meaning or are directed at you in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do feel that way, though again I want to be careful with the phrase “special meaning,” because that makes it sound like I’m attaching some kind of fantasy meaning to ordinary things. To me it’s more practical than that. It’s not that every object or every event in the world is about me. I’m not saying that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is that in my building, for months now, there have been these repeated things — mail going to the wrong place or showing up in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls at certain times, neighbors making small comments that seem casual if you don’t know the background — and they line up too specifically with what’s been happening. I’m here because I believe the property manager and certain people in the building are using those things to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd when I bring it up, there’s always some explanation ready. Paperwork, maintenance, “oh, that was a mistake,” “oh, that’s just normal building noise,” whatever it is. But the explanations don’t change the pattern. If anything, the way they always have an explanation ready is part of what makes it so obvious to me.\n\nSo yes, I do think certain actions by others are directed at me. Not everything, not random things on the street, but the things happening around where I live — yes. Those are not just unrelated events to me. They’re connected, and they’re meant to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really outside the building. I don’t watch the news and think they’re talking to me, or see something on television and think it has some message meant for me. That’s not what this is.

What I’m talking about is much more specific. It’s centered around where I live. It’s the property manager and certain people in the building, and the things that have been happening there for months — the mail being misdelivered or turning up wrong, the noises in the walls at particular times, neighbors making little comments that sound casual but fit too neatly with what’s been going on. That’s where the pattern is.

So if I’m in a store or watching TV, no, I don’t feel like strangers or the news are sending me signals. But in the building, yes, I do believe those things are directed at me. And people keep acting like if each thing has an explanation — paperwork, maintenance, “mistake,” whatever — then that settles it. But it doesn’t, because the pattern is still there. It’s been a way of pressuring me out of the apartment without saying it openly.

Not directly, of course — they’re too careful for that — but through things like my mail being misdelivered in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls that happen at very particular times, and neighbors making these little comments that connect to things they shouldn’t know or that clearly refer to what’s been happening. And then when I ask about it, there’s always paperwork or some bland explanation, like it’s all routine or accidental. But when you look at the pattern over time, it’s obvious. Months of it. I mean, I hear noises, but these are real noises in the building — pipes, tapping, things in the walls, footsteps, doors, that kind of thing. It’s not like I’m hearing some voice talking to me when nobody is there, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAnd seeing things… no, I don’t think so. I notice things other people don’t notice, but that’s different. Some people are just not paying attention, or they don’t want to get involved. I’m fairly observant — I was an analyst for years, so patterns stand out to me. But I’m not seeing, you know, figures or objects that aren’t there. It’s more that I’m picking up on details that other people dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Have you talked to others about your concerns with the building, and do they share your view or do they seem to disagree with you about what’s happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’ve talked to people, though not as many as you might think, because you learn pretty quickly who is actually listening and who is just trying to manage you. I brought it up with the property manager first, obviously, because the mail being “accidentally” misdelivered over and over again was not normal. Then there were the noises in the walls — always at these particular times, like after I’d made a complaint or when I was trying to sleep — and neighbors making these little comments in the hallway that were just a bit too specific.\n\nMost people don’t come right out and agree, no. They’ll say things like, “Oh, mail gets mixed up,” or “Old buildings make noise,” or they’ll shrug and act like they don’t know what I mean. But that’s part of the problem. They want it to seem ordinary if you take each thing separately. I’ve tried to explain that it’s the pattern over months that matters. One mistake is one thing. Two, maybe. But when it’s the same kinds of incidents, with the same people around it, and every time there’s some neat little explanation or piece of paperwork to cover it, then at a certain point you have to stop pretending it’s coincidence.\n\nSo, no, they don’t really share my view openly. Some disagree, or say I’m reading too much into it. But I think some of them know more than they admit. They just don’t want to be involved, or they’re afraid of the manager, or they’re part of it. That’s why I’m here — because I need someone outside the building to understand that this has been going on for months, and no amount of their bland explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a coordinated effort or plan by the property manager and others in the building to make you leave, and if so, how do you know this is happening?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I don’t really see how else to describe it at this point.\n\nI know that sounds like a strong word — “coordinated” — but when something happens once, fine, you can call it an accident. When the mail is misdelivered again and again, when the noises in the walls seem to come at particular times, and then certain neighbors make these little comments that line up with what’s been going on, it stops looking random. It’s been months of this.\n\nAnd the property manager always has some explanation ready. Paperwork, routine maintenance, “these things happen,” that kind of thing. But that’s exactly how they keep it deniable. Each separate thing can be explained away if you isolate it, but I’m not looking at one separate thing. I’m looking at the pattern.\n\nI worked as a management analyst for years. Patterns are what I notice. And this pattern is pressure — not direct, not written in a letter saying “leave,” obviously — but pressure to make living there uncomfortable enough that I’ll give up the apartment. The mail, the timed noises, the little comments, the bland explanations afterward… all of it fits together too neatly. No amount of their paperwork changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the property manager and others are targeting you for a specific reason, such as something about who you are, your background, or your actions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about my background in the usual sense, no. I mean, I’m not saying it’s because of my age or where I’m from or anything like that. It’s more specific than that. I think it’s because I notice things, and because I don’t just let sloppy explanations pass when the pattern doesn’t make sense.\n\nOnce I started pointing out the mail problem, and then the noises, and then the way certain neighbors would make these little comments that were just a little too well-timed, I think I became inconvenient. That’s the simplest way to put it. They want me out of the apartment, and they don’t want to do it directly because then there would be a record. So instead it’s this steady pressure — misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, comments in the hallway, and then the property manager produces some paperwork or bland explanation to make each incident look ordinary.\n\nBut I’m telling you, over months, it isn’t ordinary. You can dress it up however you want, but the pattern is still there. I’m here because I need someone to understand that. It’s not one thing. It’s the accumulation of things, and no amount of their explanations changes what I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe this coordinated effort against you is part of a larger plan or organization that extends beyond just the people in your building?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I would say it’s some large organization, if that’s what you mean. I’m not claiming there’s a whole outside group with meetings and letterhead or anything like that. What I can say is what I’ve actually seen in the building, over and over, for months.\n\nThe property manager and certain people there are involved enough for it to be coordinated. That’s the word I keep coming back to, because the mail being misdelivered repeatedly, the noises in the walls at particular times, and then those little comments from neighbors — comments that seem casual if you weren’t paying attention, but are just too specific — all of that fits together. And then afterward there’s always some explanation, some paperwork, some “routine” reason to make it look normal.\n\nSo beyond the building? I can’t prove that, and I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is. But inside the building, yes, absolutely, there is a pattern. They’re trying to pressure me out of the apartment without saying it directly. And no amount of bland explanations changes what I can see when I look at all of it together.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your situation in the building is part of something bigger, like being watched or targeted because of something you know or who you are in a more significant way than just being an inconvenience to the property manager?\n\nPatient: No, not in that larger sense. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that I know some secret, or that there’s some outside group watching me because of who I am. That’s not what I’m trying to say.\n\nWhat I am saying is narrower, but it’s still very real. I’m here because for months now the property manager and certain people in the building have been using these things — the mail being misdelivered, the noises in the walls that happen at particular times, the little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s been happening — to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd every time I raise it, there’s an explanation ready. Paperwork, routine issue, mistake, maintenance, some perfectly bland answer. But those explanations only work if you look at each incident by itself. I’m not looking at one envelope or one noise or one hallway comment. I’m looking at the pattern over months.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t call it something bigger like I’m being targeted because I know something important. I think I became inconvenient to them, and now they’re trying to make living there uncomfortable enough that I leave on my own. That’s the part I need someone to understand. Their paperwork doesn’t change the pattern I can see.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the property manager or others in the building have abilities or powers that others don’t, like being able to control events in ways that seem impossible, or know things they couldn’t normally know?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t put it that way. I’m not saying they have powers or some impossible ability. I don’t think the property manager can control things supernaturally or read my mind or anything like that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is much more practical, but it’s still deliberate. They’re in a position where they can affect things around me — mail, maintenance, access to certain areas, communication with neighbors, that kind of thing. And when you have a property manager and certain people in the building acting together, they don’t need special powers. They just need coordination.\n\nThat’s what I keep trying to explain. For months now, it’s been misdelivered mail, noises in the walls at particular times, and those little comments from neighbors that line up too neatly with what’s going on. Then when I ask about it or push back, suddenly there’s paperwork, or a routine explanation, or some excuse that makes each thing sound harmless if you isolate it.\n\nBut I’m not looking at each thing in isolation. I’m looking at the pattern. And no, that doesn’t require impossible abilities. It requires access, timing, and people willing to make my life uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. That’s what I believe is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the actions taken against you by the property manager and others in the building are planned and intentional, even if they may seem minor or explainable on their own?\n\nPatient: Yes. Yes, I do believe that. And I know someone could take any one thing I’m talking about and say, “Well, mail gets misdelivered,” or “Buildings make noises,” or “Neighbors say odd things sometimes.” I understand that. I’m not naïve about that.\n\nBut this has been going on for months, and it’s the way the pieces line up that matters. The mail doesn’t just go missing or end up somewhere else once. The noises in the walls happen at particular times, not just randomly. And then there are these little comments from people in the building — small things, things they can pretend are nothing — but they connect too closely to what’s been happening.\n\nThat’s why I say it’s intentional. Not because each incident is dramatic on its own, but because the pattern is too consistent. And every time I raise it, there’s paperwork or some routine explanation ready to make it sound normal. That’s part of it too, in my view. They want each piece to look minor so I sound unreasonable if I complain.\n\nBut I can see what they’re doing. The property manager and certain people in the building are using these things to pressure me out of the apartment without openly saying that’s what they’re doing. No amount of explanations changes that pattern for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one month where you experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things others don’t, or having thoughts that are very disorganized or hard to follow, and this wasn’t due to substance use or another medical condition?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d describe anything in those terms. I mean, “hearing voices” or “seeing things” — no, not in the way I think you mean, like some separate voice talking to me or seeing a person standing there who isn’t there. That’s not really what this is about.\n\nAs far as my thoughts being disorganized, I don’t think so. I know I can go into a lot of detail, and maybe I circle back because people keep missing the point, but to me it’s connected. I’m following a sequence of events. Other people may not want to see the connection, but that’s different from my thoughts not making sense.\n\nAnd I don’t use substances in a way that would explain anything like that. I’m just trying to be precise because when I give a short answer, people act like I’m leaving something out, and when I give the whole explanation, they say I’m overexplaining. So… no, I don’t believe I’ve had a month like what you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that certain events happening around you — like the noises, misdelivered mail, or things people say — have a special or hidden meaning that’s specifically directed at you, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t use the word “special” exactly, because that makes it sound mystical or like I’m imagining symbols in everything. That’s not what I mean. But do I believe those things are directed at me? Yes. Absolutely.\n\nThe misdelivered mail, the noises in the walls at certain times, the little comments from neighbors that seem casual but line up too neatly with what’s been happening — those aren’t just random to me. Other people may look at each one separately and say, “Oh, that’s nothing,” but that’s the problem. They’re designed to look like nothing if you take them one at a time.\n\nTo me the meaning is pretty clear: the property manager and certain people in the building are trying to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave the apartment on my own. They don’t say it openly, of course. There’s always some paperwork or routine explanation ready if I question it. But the pattern has been going on for months, and I can see it.\n\nSo yes, even if other people don’t see it, I believe those events are directed at me. Not in some supernatural way, but in a coordinated, intentional way.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered that others may not see the pattern the way you do, and do you think it’s possible that these events are not connected the way you believe?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered that other people may not see it, yes. I mean, I’m sitting here talking to you because I understand that from the outside, if someone just hears “misdelivered mail” or “noises in the walls” or “a neighbor made a comment,” they can say, “Well, those things happen.” I do understand that.\n\nBut the issue is that they’re not isolated things to me. This has been going on for months, and it’s the timing and the repetition and the way the explanations are always ready afterward. The property manager and certain people in the building can point to paperwork or routine procedures and make each piece sound normal, but when you’re the one living there, day after day, you can see how it all fits together.\n\nSo do I think it’s possible they’re not connected? Honestly, no — not in any real way. I can say, intellectually, that other people may not see the pattern because they aren’t experiencing the whole thing. But for me, the pattern is clear. It’s been a pressure campaign to make me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without them having to say that directly. And no amount of their explanations changes what I can see happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events, objects, or actions by others — even things that aren’t directly related to you — have a special meaning or are directed at you in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do feel that way, though again I want to be careful with the phrase “special meaning,” because that makes it sound like I’m attaching some kind of fantasy meaning to ordinary things. I’m not saying that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is that in my building, for months now, there have been these repeated things — mail going to the wrong place or showing up in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls at certain times, neighbors making small comments that seem casual if you don’t know the background — and they line up too specifically with what’s been happening. I’m here because I believe the property manager and certain people in the building are using those things to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd when I bring it up, there’s always some explanation ready. Paperwork, maintenance, “oh, that was a mistake,” “oh, that’s just normal building noise,” whatever it is. But the explanations don’t change the pattern. If anything, the way they always have an explanation ready is part of what makes it so obvious to me.\n\nSo yes, I do think certain actions by others are directed at me. Not everything, not random things on the street, but the things happening around where I live — yes. Those are not just unrelated events to me. They’re connected, and they’re meant to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events, actions, or objects outside of your building — such as things you see on TV, in the news, or people’s actions in public — are directed at you or have a meaning specifically for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside the building. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I do think some of those things can be connected, even if the person isn’t openly tied to the property manager. I’m not saying every single person in the building is part of it, or that every footstep or every conversation is about me. But after months of this, you start to notice when certain things happen at certain times, and when certain people seem to say little things that line up too neatly with everything else.

The problem is that if you take each thing by itself, it sounds harmless. Someone is talking in the hallway. Someone moves around upstairs or next door. Mail gets put in the wrong place. There’s noise in the walls. A neighbor makes a casual comment. Fine — any one of those can be explained away. And that’s exactly what keeps happening. There’s always paperwork, or “maintenance,” or “that was just a mistake,” or “you misunderstood.” But I’m the one who has been living with it day after day, and I can see the pattern.

So yes, when people in the building do things that might look neutral to someone else, I sometimes do see a connection to what the property manager is doing. Not because I want to. I’d rather not have to think about it at all. But it’s been going on for months now — the misdelivered mail, the timed noises in the walls, the little comments from neighbors — and it feels like a coordinated way of making me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without anyone having to say that directly. Their explanations don’t really change that for me.

This has been going on for months, and it’s the timing and the repetition and the way the explanations are always ready afterward. The property manager and certain people in the building can point to paperwork or routine procedures and make each piece sound normal, but when you’re the one living there, day after day, you can see how it all fits together.\n\nSo do I think it’s possible they’re not connected? Honestly, no — not in any real way. I can say, intellectually, that other people may not see the pattern because they aren’t experiencing the whole thing. But for me, the pattern is clear. It’s been a pressure campaign to make me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without them having to say that directly. And no amount of their explanations changes what I can see happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that events, objects, or actions by others — even things that aren’t directly related to you — have a special meaning or are directed at you in some way?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do feel that way, though again I want to be careful with the phrase “special meaning,” because that makes it sound like I’m attaching some kind of fantasy meaning to ordinary things. To me it’s more practical than that. It’s not that every object or every event in the world is about me. I’m not saying that.\n\nWhat I’m saying is that in my building, for months now, there have been these repeated things — mail going to the wrong place or showing up in ways that don’t make sense, noises in the walls at certain times, neighbors making small comments that seem casual if you don’t know the background — and they line up too specifically with what’s been happening. I’m here because I believe the property manager and certain people in the building are using those things to pressure me out of the apartment.\n\nAnd when I bring it up, there’s always some explanation ready. Paperwork, maintenance, “oh, that was a mistake,” “oh, that’s just normal building noise,” whatever it is. But the explanations don’t change the pattern. If anything, the way they always have an explanation ready is part of what makes it so obvious to me.\n\nSo yes, I do think certain actions by others are directed at me. Not everything, not random things on the street, but the things happening around where I live — yes. Those are not just unrelated events to me. They’re connected, and they’re meant to make me uncomfortable enough that I leave.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that events, actions, or objects outside of your building — such as things you see on TV, in the news, or people’s actions in public — are directed at you or have a meaning specifically for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really outside the building. I don’t watch the news and think they’re talking to me, or see something on television and think it has some message meant for me. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is much more specific. It’s centered around where I live. It’s the property manager and certain people in the building, and the things that have been happening there for months — the mail being misdelivered or turning up wrong, the noises in the walls at particular times, neighbors making little comments that sound casual but fit too neatly with what’s been going on. That’s where the pattern is.\n\nSo if I’m in a store or watching TV, no, I don’t feel like strangers or the news are sending me signals. But in the building, yes, I do believe those things are directed at me. It’s been a way of pressuring me out of the apartment without saying it openly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that neutral or unrelated actions by people in the building—like their everyday movements or conversations—have a hidden connection or meaning related to you, even if they aren’t directly involved with the property manager?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think some of those things can be connected, even if the person isn’t openly tied to the property manager. I’m not saying every single person in the building is part of it, or that every footstep or every conversation is about me. But after months of this, you start to notice when certain things happen at certain times, and when certain people seem to say little things that line up too neatly with everything else.\n\nThe problem is that if you take each thing by itself, it sounds harmless. Someone is talking in the hallway. Someone moves around upstairs or next door. Mail gets put in the wrong place. There’s noise in the walls. A neighbor makes a casual comment. Fine — any one of those can be explained away. And that’s exactly what keeps happening. There’s always paperwork, or “maintenance,” or “that was just a mistake,” or “you misunderstood.” But I’m the one who has been living with it day after day, and I can see the pattern.\n\nSo yes, when people in the building do things that might look neutral to someone else, I sometimes do see a connection to what the property manager is doing. Not because I want to. I’d rather not have to think about it at all. But it’s been going on for months now — the misdelivered mail, the timed noises in the walls, the little comments from neighbors — and it feels like a coordinated way of making me uncomfortable enough to leave the apartment without anyone having to say that directly. Their explanations don’t really change that for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
